### SB-0023 — Live Google Maps API key is in PUBLIC git history and was never rotated — the same key is in mobile/.env today

- **status** `confirmed` · **area** `mobile` · found 2026-08-19 by audit-wf-72374f5f · checked 2026-08-19
- **files** `mobile/app.json:28 (in blobs cb5420afc4 / 9a8dbc70a7 / e3b3b156aa; commits 5c51dd0..000524b)`, `mobile/.env (untracked, holds the identical key)`, `mobile/app.config.js:11`, `mobile/.env.example`
- **evidence** [overnight audit resume wf_72374f5f — dimension: secrets and credential hygiene] code_state=committed confidence=confirmed NEEDS-HUMAN

gh repo view 4alkubati/SWINGBY --json visibility → {"isPrivate":false,"visibility":"PUBLIC"}

Commit 5c51dd0 (2026-05-29), message: "Hardening waves 1-12: ... real Maps key, Sentry DSNs"
Blob cb5420afc4, path mobile/app.json, line 28 (value masked by me):
    "googleMapsApiKey": "AIza<REDACTED_39CHAR_GOOGLE_KEY>",

The key survives in 3 distinct blobs (9a8dbc70a7, cb5420afc4, e3b3b156aa) across 5 commits, removed only at 000524b (2026-07-01, "Maps key placeholder").

All five commits are published:
  $ for c in 5c51dd0 f0d7635 57e960b 000524b 4820da5; do git merge-base --is-ancestor $c origin/main; done
  5c51dd0  reachable-from-origin/main: YES
  f0d7635  reachable-from-origin/main: YES
  57e960b  reachable-from-origin/main: YES
  000524b  reachable-from-origin/main: YES

  f0d7635 chore: sync local work to GitHub ...
  5c51dd0 Hardening waves 1-12: ... real Maps key, Sentry DSNs

NOT ROTATED — SHA-256 comparison against the key live in mobile/.env today:
  leaked key sha256[:12] = 6305d8bba3b6
  mobile/.env: 1 AIza key(s)
     sha256[:12]=6305d8bba3b6  SAME_AS_LEAKED=True

Key shape: len=39, 25 distinct chars after the AIza prefix, zero placeholder markers (no 'your'/'dummy'/'xxx'/'example'). mobile/.env.example calls this variable "THE ONE PLACE TO PASTE THE REAL KEY".

CONSEQUENCE: Anyone who clones or browses github.com/4alkubati/SWINGBY can recover a working Google Maps/Geocoding API key from history in seconds — automated GitHub secret scanners already do this continuously. The key is used both by the mobile app (mobile/.env → app.config.js:11) and server-side by backend/app/services/geocoding.py. Consequences run from billed quota theft (Geocoding/Places are metered per request against the project's card) to quota exhaustion that renders every map blank and silently kills server-side geocoding of new job posts. Removing it from HEAD at 000524b did nothing — a git blob stays fetchable forever once pushed.
- **verify** `Three checks, all must pass. Replaces the stored step, whose part (a) has a false-pass mode (if mobile/.env is missing or has no key, `re.search(...).group(0)` raises AttributeError and the script "does not print 6305d8bba3b6" — which reads as a pass) and whose part (b) needlessly punts the decisive check to a human console when it is fully automatable.

(a) The OLD key is DEAD at Google — this is the only check that proves remediation, and it needs no console. Pull the leaked value straight out of the public blob and probe it read-only:
  cd /home/l3thal/brain/10-swingby/swingby && git cat-file -p cb5420afc4 | python3 -c "
import sys,re,json,urllib.request,urllib.parse
k=re.search(r'AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{35}',sys.stdin.read()).group(0)
r=json.load(urllib.request.urlopen('https://maps.googleapis.com/maps/api/geocode/json?address=Calgary&key='+urllib.parse.quote(k),timeout=20))
print(r.get('status'))"
  → MUST print REQUEST_DENIED (today it prints OK). Any output of OK, or ZERO_RESULTS, or OVER_QUERY_LIMIT means the key still authenticates and the finding is NOT fixed.

(b) The local dev key is a different key, with an explicit failure if the file is missing:
  cd /home/l3thal/brain/10-swingby/swingby && python3 -c "
import re,hashlib,sys
s=open('mobile/.env').read()
m=re.search(r'AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{35}',s)
assert m, 'FAIL: no GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY in mobile/.env — inconclusive, not a pass'
print(hashlib.sha256(m.group(0).encode()).hexdigest()[:12])"
  → must print something OTHER than 6305d8bba3b6, and must not raise.

(c) The replacement is rotated everywhere the old one lived, not just locally — `eas env:list` must show GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY updated (as a secret) in both `preview` and `production`, and the Render backend env must be updated. Record the rotation date and the new key's restrictions as a row in docs/SECURITY.md; that row's existence is the artifact to check.

Optional (d), cleanup only, never a substitute for (a):
  git log --all --format=%H --find-object=cb5420afc4 -- mobile/app.json   # must output nothing
  gh api repos/4alkubati/SWINGBY/contents/mobile/app.json?ref=5c51dd0 --jq .sha   # must 404`
- **fix** Rotate first, purge second — in that order, because rotation is the only step that actually revokes access. (1) In Google Cloud console create a NEW key for project swingby-503604, apply Android package + SHA-1 / iOS bundle-id / HTTP-referrer restrictions and an API allowlist plus a quota cap, then DELETE the old key. (2) Update mobile/.env, the EAS `preview` and `production` environments (`eas env:update --name GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY`), and the backend Render env. (3) Optionally rewrite history (git-filter-repo --replace-text) and force-push, but treat that as cleanup, not remediation. (4) Enable GitHub push protection / secret scanning on the repo. (5) Add a row to docs/SECURITY.md recording the leak, the rotation date, and the new key's restrictions — there is currently no third-party credential inventory there at all.

### SB-0091 — The leaked Maps key is ALSO the EAS Places key — it ships inlined in every app bundle, a third publication route

- **status** `open` · **area** `mobile` · found 2026-08-20 by fix-session-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `mobile/eas.json`, `mobile/.env`
- **repro** cd mobile && npx eas-cli env:list preview   # EXPO_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_PLACES_KEY is printed in full
- **evidence** Found 2026-08-20 while checking build readiness. 'eas env:list preview' shows EXPO_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_PLACES_KEY=AIzaSyDW2h2noMJ_PyVCwub17DIwi8xb8BAnyJw in PLAINTEXT. Its sha256[:12] is 6305d8bba3b6 — byte-identical to the key in public git history AND to the key in mobile/.env. All three are one key. Anything named EXPO_PUBLIC_* is inlined into the published JS bundle by Expo, so every preview build made since that variable was set carries the key inside the app, readable by anyone who unzips the IPA. That is a third publication route on top of git history and the .env. It is also precisely the trap SB-0029 was filed about — PostJobScreen's removed fallback to EXPO_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY — except it was already realised through the Places variable rather than the Maps one. Note GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY is correctly stored as an EAS SECRET (unreadable, native-only); only the Places variable is public by design.
- **verify** `The Places key must be a DIFFERENT key from the Maps key and from anything in git history. Check: cd mobile && npx eas-cli env:list preview | grep PLACES  — then sha256 that value and confirm it is NOT 6305d8bba3b6, and that a Geocoding call with the OLD key returns REQUEST_DENIED.`
- **fix** Rotation must replace the key in FOUR places, not one: (1) create a new native Maps key and a SEPARATE Places-only key in Google Cloud, (2) delete the old key — this is the step that revokes, (3) mobile/.env, (4) eas env:update for BOTH GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY (secret) and EXPO_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_PLACES_KEY (public, so it must be the restricted Places-only key). Then rebuild; the current build embeds the compromised key.

### SB-0095 — Tapping a stale auth link while already logged in can wipe the real session

- **status** `open` · **area** `mobile` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `mobile/src/services/authLink.js:134-148`
- **evidence** [WRONG ALGORITHM] `useAuthDeepLink` (mobile/App.js:65) is mounted unconditionally in `RootNavigator`, running whether or not `user` is already set — but `completeAuthFromUrl`'s own header comment claims "A confirmation link arrives precisely when the user is logged OUT" and the code never checks that. If a logged-in user (or anyone testing multiple accounts on one device) opens any old/foreign `swingby://auth-callback#access_token=...` link, `storeSession()` at line 134 unconditionally overwrites the SAME `swingby_token`/`swingby_refresh_token` SecureStore keys the currently active session uses (see TOKEN_KEY/REFRESH_KEY in services/auth.js), and `setAuthToken()` swaps the axios client onto the new (possibly bad) token — before the new token is validated.

Proof: if the subsequent `getMe()` at line 138 then fails, the catch block at 140-149 deletes BOTH SecureStore keys entirely and sets `setAuthToken(null)`, permanently destroying the previously-valid session's persisted tokens. The in-memory React `user` state (owned by AuthContext, untouched here since `adoptSession` is never called on this path) still shows the old user as logged in, so nothing looks wrong until the next app restart or access-token expiry, at which point the real session can no longer be restored or refreshed and the user is silently logged out with no explanation.

--- PROVENANCE (added by triage 2026-08-20) ---
Found by the sentinel cron, which audits the WORKING TREE of ~/brain/10-swingby/swingby while computing its diff range from origin/main, and never syncs the two. From 2026-08-19 21:13 until 2026-08-20 21:58 that tree sat on fix/fabricated-social-proof @ a1e9446 — FIVE commits behind main, missing #159 and #163 ('74 of 86 findings fixed with tests'). Two findings sampled from this batch (HEIC upload sniff, expiry-sweep statuses) were already fixed on main and their proofs were false there. UNVERIFIED AGAINST main — re-check before fixing.
STALE-RISK: lower — no cited file changed between a1e9446 and main.
- **verify** `if the subsequent `getMe()` at line 138 then fails, the catch block at 140-149 deletes BOTH SecureStore keys entirely and sets `setAuthToken(null)`, permanently destroying the previously-valid session's persisted tokens. The in-memory React `user` state (owned by AuthContext, untouched here since `adoptSession` is never called on this path) still shows the old user as logged in, so nothing looks wrong until the next app restart or access-token expiry, at which point the real session can no longer be restored or refreshed and the user is silently logged out with no explanation.`
- **fix** before calling `storeSession`/`setAuthToken` in `completeAuthFromUrl`, snapshot the existing tokens and restore them (instead of deleting both keys outright) if `getMe()` on the new token fails — or have `useAuthDeepLink` skip processing entirely while `user` is already set.

### SB-0103 — Invoice totals are computed from the booking's frozen fields, ignoring the actual payments ledger loaded for the same invoice

- **status** `open` · **area** `backend` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `backend/app/api/invoices.py:160-194`
- **evidence** [WRONG NUMBERS] The invoice endpoint fetches the `payments` row (including `total_charged_cents`/`platform_cut_cents`/`released_to_business_cents`) but only uses it to compute a `capture_backed` flag — every dollar figure on the invoice (`total_amount`, `platform_cut`, `to_business`) is instead derived from `bookings.total_amount_cents`/`platform_fee_cents`, frozen at accept time. There is no status gate excluding cancelled bookings, so a cancelled booking's invoice still shows the pre-cancellation 10%/90% split even though the real ledger (per `cancel_booking`, which sets `platform_cut=0` and `released_to_business=business_keeps` — 25% or 50%) says otherwise.

Proof: invoices.py:160-171 selects `total_charged`, `platform_cut`, `released_to_business` (and their `_cents` twins) into `payment`, but lines 178-194 build `total_c`/`cut_c`/`to_business_c` entirely from `booking.get("total_amount_cents")`/`booking.get("platform_fee_cents")` — `payment` is never referenced again except at line 199's `capture_backed = escrow.is_capture_backed(payment)`. `backend/tests/test_invoices.py`'s fixture sets `booking.total_amount == payment.total_charged`, masking the divergence.

### SB-0107 — Admin's "cases need a call" badge counts all open disputes but the copy claims every one is a money hold

- **evidence** [WRONG NUMBERS] `pending` is set from `GET /disputes/admin/queue`'s `count`, which is every dispute with status `open`/`under_review` regardless of `issue_type` — ordinary complaints (`no_show`, `poor_quality`, `damage`, `overcharge`, `safety`, `other`) count exactly the same as a `cancellation_refund`. The card then describes the whole number as escrowed money awaiting a photo/voice-memo review, which is only true of the `cancellation_refund` subset.

Proof: `disputes.py`'s `admin_dispute_queue` builds `items` from `.in_("status", ["open", "under_review"])` with no `issue_type` filter, and only sets `needs_money_decision = issue_type == CANCELLATION_REFUND and held > 0` per-row — `count = len(items)` includes both. `AdminScreen.js:72-74` renders `` `${pending} cases need a call. Cancelled jobs where work had already started hold the client's money until you approve or decline the refund — you decide from the before/after photos and the voice memo.` `` for that same `pending` number, with no split between money and non-money cases. A queue of, say, 2 `safety` disputes and 0 `cancellation_refund`s shows "2 cases need a call... hold the client's money" when zero dollars are actually held.

### SB-0108 — Business cancellation screen tells a provider "a penalty applies to you" for a figure the backend never actually charges

- **status** `open` · **area** `mobile` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `mobile/src/screens/flows/CancellationFlowScreen.js:187-204,`, `mobile/src/screens/business/JobManagementScreen.js:580-593`
- **evidence** [COPYWRITING] When a business cancels late/no-show, the new business half of `CancellationFlowScreen` shows a dollar "Penalty To You" (25%/50% of the job) and the toast says "A $X.XX penalty applies to you" — and `JobManagementScreen`'s new "Cancel this job" row warns "a penalty may apply to you". But `escrow.compute_cancellation_split`'s `business_penalty_c` (the figure both screens mirror) is never written into the payments ledger for a business cancel — `bookings.py`'s `cancel_booking` writes `released_to_business=split["business_keeps_cents"]`, and `business_keeps_c` stays 0 in every timing branch when `actor == "business"` (escrow.py:414, only set inside the `actor == "client"` branch). The business already gets $0 of the escrow regardless of whether it's early, late, or a no-show — the 25%/50% number is what escrow.py's own docstring labels "audit only," recorded solely on `cancellations.penalty_amount` with no code anywhere that debits it from a payout, deposit or balance.

Proof: `escrow.py:396` — `business_penalty — dollars the business is penalised (audit only)`. `escrow.py:430-438` — the `actor == "business"` branch never touches `business_keeps_c` (stays 0); it only sets `business_penalty_c`. `bookings.py:1346-1350` — the ledger write for the request path uses `released_to_business=split["business_keeps_cents"]` (0), never `business_penalty_cents`. `grep -rn "business_penalty" backend/app` (excluding tests) shows `business_penalty_cents`/`business_penalty` used nowhere but `escrow.py`'s own dict construction — no charge, no deduction, no other reader.

- **evidence** [WRONG ALGORITHM] This diff added a `hidden_at`/`blocked_pair_ids` filter to `list_open_posts` (and interests.py's `express_interest` got the identical guard, with a comment explicitly reasoning "hiding a post from a feed is not enforcement — a business holding the post id could otherwise still quote on it"). `get_service_post`, the single-post-by-id read in the same file, was never given either check, so the exact bypass that reasoning was written to close is still open for reads: an admin can hide a post for hate speech/harassment via `PATCH /moderation/reports/{id}/resolve` with `action_taken=content_hidden`, and anyone who already has the post id (a saved link, a push notification, a thread reference) can still `GET /service_posts/{id}` and see it in full, unmasked if they're the owner, masked-but-present otherwise.

Proof: `list_open_posts` (service_posts.py:428-431,443-445) now does `if post.get("hidden_at"): continue` and `if post.get("client_id") in blocked: continue`; `express_interest` (interests.py:66-73) does the same two checks and 404s. `get_service_post` (service_posts.py:469-496) selects `"*"` (so `hidden_at` is right there in `res.data`) but never reads it, and never calls `blocked_pair_ids` at all — it only gates on `target_business_id` ownership before returning the row.

### SB-0119 — Report mechanism ships for 2 of the 6 UGC surfaces it claims to cover

- **status** `open` · **area** `backend` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified

No screen anywhere imports `REPORT_TARGETS.SERVICE_POST`, `.BOOKING_PHOTO`, or `.VOICE_NOTE` — there is no long-press, overflow menu, or button on a job post, a proof-of-work photo, or a voice note anywhere in the app that lets a user report it.

Proof: `git grep -ln "ReportSheet" mobile/src` returns only `ReportSheet.js` itself, its test, `BusinessProfileScreen.js`, and `ChatScreen.js`. `git grep "REPORT_TARGETS\." mobile/src` (excluding the definition file) returns exactly 4 call sites, covering only `REVIEW`, `BUSINESS`, `USER`, and `MESSAGE`. `mobile/src/i18n.js:1213` (`moderation.reportPost`) and the `BOOKING_PHOTO`/`VOICE_NOTE` entries in `REPORT_TARGETS` (services/moderation.js:29,32-33) are unreferenced by any screen.

### SB-0121 — Two of three "Cancel booking" entry points never pass the price, so the fee preview always reads $0 / full refund

- **status** `open` · **area** `mobile` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `mobile/src/screens/client/BookingDetailsScreen.js:627-632,`, `mobile/src/screens/client/ActiveBookingScreen.js:711-715`
- **evidence** [WRONG NUMBERS] `CancellationFlowScreen` computes the penalty preview from a `quotedPrice` route param that defaults to `0`, and a `scheduledDate` param that (when absent) makes `classifyTiming` return `'no_date'` (0% penalty). `BookingDetailsScreen.handleCancel` navigates with `{ bookingId, scheduledDate }` — no `quotedPrice`, so `amount` is always `parseFloat(0 * pct).toFixed(2)` = `$0.00` regardless of actual timing. `ActiveBookingScreen`'s cancel button is worse: it passes only `{ bookingId }`, omitting `scheduledDate` too, so `classifyTiming(undefined)` always returns `'no_date'` and the preview shows "Cancel booking — full refund" even inside the 48h/no-show window. Only the business-side call in `JobManagementScreen.js:586-590` (added in the same commit as the cancel-ladder fix) passes both `scheduledDate` and `quotedPrice`.

Proof: `CancellationFlowScreen.js:79-98` `computePenalty(scheduledDateISO, quotedPrice, ...)` with `const price = parseFloat(quotedPrice) || 0`. `BookingDetailsScreen.js:627-632` — `navigation.navigate('CancellationFlow', { bookingId, scheduledDate: booking?.scheduled_at })`, no `quotedPrice` key at all. `ActiveBookingScreen.js:711-715` — `navigation.navigate('CancellationFlow', { bookingId: booking.id })`, no `scheduledDate` or `quotedPrice`. The server's actual charge (`res.penalty_amount`) is correct and used in the post-cancel toast, but the screen a client acts on before confirming always tells them the cancellation is free.

### SB-0122 — Cancellation penalty preview classifies timing off an unconfirmed proposed date, disagreeing with the backend's confirmed-date-only rule

- **status** `open` · **area** `mobile` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `mobile/src/screens/client/BookingDetailsScreen.js:566,`, `mobile/src/screens/business/JobManagementScreen.js:588`
- **evidence** [WRONG NUMBERS] `classify_cancellation_timing` (backend) reads only `bookings.confirmed_date` — with no confirmed date it always returns `'no_date'` (0% penalty either way). But both mobile call sites feed `CancellationFlowScreen` a `scheduledDate` that falls back to `proposed_date_1` (a business-proposed, not-yet-agreed date) whenever `confirmed_date` is null. A booking sits in status `'confirmed'` (which `canCancel`/the "Cancel this job" row both treat as cancellable) precisely during the window where a date has been proposed but not agreed — `interests.py:366` sets status `'confirmed'` with no `confirmed_date` yet. If that proposed date happens to be within 48h or already past, the preview shows a 25%/50% fee tier that the server will never actually charge, since it only ever sees `confirmed_date = null` and classifies the same cancel as `'no_date'` → 0%.

Proof: `escrow.classify_cancellation_timing` is called with `booking.get("confirmed_date")` only (`bookings.py:1291`). `BookingDetailsScreen.js:566` — `scheduled_at: bookingData.confirmed_date || bookingData.proposed_date_1 || null`. `JobManagementScreen.js:588` — `scheduledDate: booking.confirmed_date || booking.proposed_date_1`. `CancellationFlowScreen.js:69-77`'s `classifyTiming` has no way to distinguish a confirmed date from a proposed one — it treats whatever ISO string it's given as authoritative.

--- PROVENANCE (added by triage 2026-08-20) ---
Found by the sentinel cron, which audits the WORKING TREE of ~/brain/10-swingby/swingby while computing its diff range from origin/main, and never syncs the two. From 2026-08-19 21:13 until 2026-08-20 21:58 that tree sat on fix/fabricated-social-proof @ a1e9446 — FIVE commits behind main, missing #159 and #163 ('74 of 86 findings fixed with tests'). Two findings sampled from this batch (HEIC upload sniff, expiry-sweep statuses) were already fixed on main and their proofs were false there. UNVERIFIED AGAINST main — re-check before fixing.
STALE-RISK: HIGH — a cited file changed between a1e9446 and main.
- **verify** ``escrow.classify_cancellation_timing` is called with `booking.get("confirmed_date")` only (`bookings.py:1291`). `BookingDetailsScreen.js:566` — `scheduled_at: bookingData.confirmed_date || bookingData.proposed_date_1 || null`. `JobManagementScreen.js:588` — `scheduledDate: booking.confirmed_date || booking.proposed_date_1`. `CancellationFlowScreen.js:69-77`'s `classifyTiming` has no way to distinguish a confirmed date from a proposed one — it treats whatever ISO string it's given as authoritative.`
- **fix** pass `confirmed_date` only (never fall back to `proposed_date_1`) into `CancellationFlowScreen`'s `scheduledDate` param, so the preview's timing tier can only ever match what the backend will actually classify.

Proof: `RefundReviewScreen.js` "Approving in full sends NO amount"; `disputes.py:71` `refund_amount: float | None = None`. `disputes.py:397-400` writes the dispute row's own `refund_amount` from `moved_cents` (correct). `disputes.py:432` `record_audit(..., metadata={"refund_amount": body.refund_amount}, ...)` uses the raw, usually-`None` request body value instead.

### SB-0126 — A client's goodwill credit is granted, promised in the cancel copy, and then invisible everywhere in the app

- **status** `open` · **area** `backend` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `backend/app/api/me.py:242-271,`
- **evidence** [DEAD BUTTON] When a business cancels late/no-show, `credits.grant_credit` accrues a real $25 credit in `user_credits`, and `CancellationFlowScreen`'s business-side copy explicitly tells the provider "they receive a $25.00 credit for the late notice." `GET /me/credits` is the only endpoint that reads that ledger back (`balance_cents`, `balance`, `history`), but no screen in the mobile app calls it — grep for `/me/credits`, `balance_cents`, or `creditBalance` across `mobile/src` returns zero hits outside backend-adjacent test/copy files. `credits.CREDIT_REDEMPTION_AT_CHECKOUT_ENABLED = False` also means the credit is never auto-applied at checkout. A client who is promised and granted this credit has no way, anywhere in the product, to see they have it or spend it.

Proof: `credits.py:37` `GOODWILL_CREDIT_CENTS = 2500`; `bookings.py`'s cancel path calls `credits.grant_credit(...)` on a business late/no-show cancel. `CancellationFlowScreen.js:200` — `` `...and they receive a $${credit.toFixed(2)} credit for the late notice` `` — a promise made directly to the cancelling business about what the client gets. `me.py:242-271` defines `GET /me/credits` returning `balance_cents`/`history`; `grep -rn "/me/credits\|balance_cents\|creditBalance" mobile/src` matches nothing. `credits.py:44` `CREDIT_REDEMPTION_AT_CHECKOUT_ENABLED = False`.

### SB-0127 — Tab-bar unread badge and Dashboard's own unread pill permanently disagree on cancelled-booking threads

- **status** `open` · **area** `backend` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified

- **evidence** [WRONG NUMBERS] `GET /messages/threads` (feeds `MessagesScreen` and `DashboardScreen`'s "N unread" pill) drops any booking whose `status` isn't `confirmed/in_progress/completed`, so a cancelled booking's thread disappears from the inbox entirely. `GET /messages/unread-count` (feeds the `BottomNav` tab-bar badge via `UnreadContext`) calls `_accessible_thread_ids`, which selects bookings with no status filter at all, so it keeps counting unread messages on that same cancelled thread. `cancel_booking` never writes `messages.read_at`, so once a booking with an unread message gets cancelled, the tab badge counts it forever with no thread anywhere in the app left to open and clear it — while the Dashboard's own unread pill, built from the filtered `/threads` list, shows a different (lower) number for the same account at the same moment.

Proof: `messages.py:1063-1064` — `if b.get("status") not in ("confirmed", "in_progress", "completed"): continue` (used by both `list_threads` and `DashboardScreen.js:162-165`'s `nextUnread` sum). `messages.py:1166-1196` (`unread_count`) — `_accessible_thread_ids` selects `bookings` by `client_id`/`business_id` only (`messages.py:527-533`, no `.eq("status", ...)`), then counts unread messages across ALL those booking ids with no exclusion. `BottomNav.js:117` — `const { totalUnread } = useUnread()`, sourced from `/messages/unread-count` (`UnreadContext.js:16`).

### SB-0129 — Business Dashboard's "This week" earnings hero counts gross, not-yet-earned money, disagreeing with the "Money in Flight" card directly beneath it

The hero one tap away from both therefore shows a bigger, wrong number for "this week's earnings" than either sibling screen would report for the same period.

Proof: `DashboardScreen.js:260-268` — `earningsIn` filters `b.status === 'completed' || b.status === 'confirmed'` and reduces `Number(b.total_amount) || 0` (gross, pre-cut). `DashboardScreen.js:476-508`'s "Money in Flight" card, comment at line 475: "real numbers from GET /payments/mine only. payments === null means 'not loaded' and is never shown as $0." `EarningsScreen.js:46` sums `p.released_to_business` only.

### SB-0135 — Escrow milestone list shows two different dollar figures for what is now one atomic release

Worse, each row renders a different dollar amount for what is a single event: the "half" row shows `releasedToBusiness` (the business's 90% net, e.g. $90) and the "full" row shows `totalCharged` (the client's gross, e.g. $100), so a client sees two simultaneous checkmarks quoting two different numbers for one payment.

Proof: line 284 `const halfReleased = releasedToBusiness > 0 || ['partial', 'fully_released'].includes(payStatus);` and line 285 `const fullyReleased = payStatus === 'fully_released' && escrowHeld === 0;` — since `'partial'`/`partial_released` are legacy states no current code path writes (per CLAUDE.md: "there is no staged release any more"), `releasedToBusiness > 0` and `payStatus === 'fully_released'` flip true in the same update. Lines 318-325 then render `${releasedToBusiness.toFixed(2)}` next to the "half" row and `${totalCharged.toFixed(2)}` next to the "full" row — two different figures (net vs. gross) appearing at once.

### SB-0137 — ChatScreen's new shared quote-status call still can't detect an expired quote — the fields it reads don't exist in the API response

- **status** `open` · **area** `mobile` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `mobile/src/screens/messages/ChatScreen.js:802-808`
- **evidence** [WRONG ALGORITHM] This diff replaced ChatScreen's inline quote-status check with a call to the shared `resolveQuoteStatus` specifically to add the clock-based expiry check ChatScreen was missing (per the new test's own comment, "the exact case ChatScreen missed"). But the interest/post objects it builds read `threadInfo.created_at`, `threadInfo.post_expires_at` and `threadInfo.expires_at` — none of which `GET /messages/interest/{id}` ever returns. `resolveQuoteStatus`'s clock check silently no-ops, so a quote whose window has elapsed but whose post is still `open`/`matched` still renders as live/actionable in the thread — the identical bug this diff was written to close, just now hidden behind the shared helper.

Proof: `backend/app/api/messages.py`'s `get_interest_messages` builds the `"interest"` dict returned to the client from exactly nine keys — `id, status, quoted_price, business_id, business_name, business_logo, post_title, post_status, counterpart_user_id` — no `created_at`, no `expires_at`, no `post_expires_at`. `ChatScreen.js:803` passes `created_at: threadInfo.created_at` (always `undefined`); `:806` passes `expires_at: threadInfo.post_expires_at || threadInfo.expires_at` (both always `undefined`). Inside `resolveQuoteStatus`, `quoteExpiry(interest, post)` therefore falls through both its `post?.expires_at` and `interest?.created_at` branches and returns `null`, so `hoursLeft(null)` is `null` and the `left <= 0` expiry check never fires.

### SB-0144 — Two "How It Works" pages state the cancellation-penalty tiers shifted by one bracket, telling each side the wrong thing about their own money

- **evidence** [COPYWRITING] The real ladder (`escrow.compute_cancellation_split`) is: client cancels >48h out = 0% penalty (business gets nothing); ≤48h = 25%; no-show = 50%. Business cancels any time = client refunded 100%, with only a non-charged "penalty" figure of 0/25/50% used for audit purposes. `HowItWorksBusinesses.jsx` tells businesses "If a client cancels: 25% penalty if they cancel more than 48 hours out, 50% within 48 hours" — shifting every bracket down one tier and promising businesses money (25% on an early cancel) they will never receive. `HowItWorksClients.jsx`'s mirror FAQ makes the same one-tier shift for a business cancellation.

Proof: `escrow.py:421-429` (client actor: `early` → `client_refund_c = total_c`, i.e. business keeps 0%; `late` → business keeps 25%; `no_show` → business keeps 50%). `HowItWorksBusinesses.jsx:45` — `'25% penalty if they cancel more than 48 hours out, 50% within 48 hours'` (should be 0%/25%/50%). `HowItWorksClients.jsx:54` — `'If they cancel more than 48 hours before the booking, they pay a 25% penalty. Within 48 hours, the penalty is 50%.'` describing a business cancellation, where the client is actually guaranteed a 100% refund regardless of timing (`escrow.py:430-431`).

### SB-0149 — Notifications Center reads a storage key nothing ever writes, so the screen is permanently empty

- **evidence** [DEAD BUTTON] The whole screen — list, unread badge, mark-as-read, tap-to-navigate — is driven by `AsyncStorage` key `swingby_notifications`. Nothing in the app ever writes a new notification into that key from outside this file (no push-received listener persists to it, no API response is stored there); the only writers are this same screen's own mark-as-read handlers, which can only rewrite whatever was already there. ### SB-0153 — Content-moderation text filter is wired to one of the six UGC surfaces its own docstring claims it covers

- **status** `open` · **area** `backend` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `backend/app/api/moderation.py:4-12,`, `backend/app/api/reviews.py:20,`, `backend/app/api/service_posts.py:51-52,123-124,`, `backend/app/api/booking_photos.py:46`
- **evidence** [WRONG ALGORITHM] The Guideline-1.2 compliance docstring states SwingBy carries UGC on six surfaces (chat, voice notes, reviews, job posts, proof-of-work photos, avatars) and that filtering "(a)" applies to all of them, but `content_moderation.screen_text()` — the hate-speech/sexual-solicitation/threat filter — is called nowhere except `messages.py`. Review comments, job-post title/description, and proof-of-work captions accept free text with zero screening: a slur or threat typed into any of those three is stored and shown to other users with no BLOCK and no auto-filed report.

### SB-0207 — PATCH /auth/me can never clear a field back to null

- **evidence** [WRONG ALGORITHM] `update_me` builds `update_data = {k: v for k, v in data.model_dump().items() if v is not None}`, silently dropping any field sent as `null` before the `.update()` call — so a client can never explicitly clear a field. `ProfileEditScreen.js` relies on exactly that: clearing the phone field sends `phone: phone.trim() || null` intending to erase the saved number.

### SB-0208 — Settings "Notifications" toggle never registers or unregisters the push token

### SB-0210 — POST /me/subscribe has no guard against a business subscribing twice

- **status** `open` · **area** `backend` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `backend/app/api/subscriptions.py:97-132`
- **evidence** [WRONG ALGORITHM] `subscribe()` always creates a brand-new Stripe Checkout Session in `mode="subscription"` — it never checks `business.get("subscription_status")`/`subscription_id` before doing so, so an already-`active` or `past_due` business can end up with two independently-billing Stripe subscriptions. `AutoBiddingScreen`'s "Update billing" CTA for `past_due` businesses calls this same endpoint instead of opening the Stripe billing portal the way `BusinessProfileScreen.js:812-817` correctly does via `manage_url`.

Proof: lines 106-132 reuse `stripe_customer_id` if present but perform zero lookup of an existing subscription before `stripe.checkout.Session.create(mode="subscription", ...)`. The webhook that reconciles state (`payments_stripe.py:427-441`) keys its `businesses` UPDATE purely on `business_id` from metadata, so a second subscription's `checkout.session.completed` event just overwrites `subscription_status`/`subscription_id` — masking that the first subscription is still live and still billing. Not currently reachable in this env (`STRIPE_PRICE_SOLO`/`STRIPE_PRICE_TEAM` are unset locally, so `subscribe()` takes the no-op "trialing" branch instead), but the guard is missing regardless of config.

STALE-RISK: lower — no cited file changed between a1e9446 and main.

Not currently reachable in this env (`STRIPE_PRICE_SOLO`/`STRIPE_PRICE_TEAM` are unset locally, so `subscribe()` takes the no-op "trialing" branch instead), but the guard is missing regardless of config.`

### SB-0218 — WorkerTrustCard rebuilt twice, still rendered nowhere

### SB-0224 — Admin "Hide the content" silently no-ops for business/user reports but still marks them permanently resolved

- **evidence** [WRONG ALGORITHM] `services/moderation.TARGETS` marks `business`/`user` reports `"hideable": False` since there's no content row to stamp `hidden_at` on, and `hide_content()` correctly returns `False` with no write for those. But `resolve_report` never inspects that return value — it always marks the report `status="resolved", action_taken="content_hidden"` (a real, un-reopenable outcome; resolving twice 409s), so an admin hiding a reported business or user profile sees "Resolved" while nothing was actually hidden. `business`/`user` are exactly the two report types already reachable in production today (BusinessProfileScreen reports business/review, ChatScreen reports message/user). The mobile Hide button is enabled unconditionally (`disabled={!!busy}`), unlike Warn/Suspend which are correctly gated by `canSuspend`.

Proof: `moderation.py` (services) lines 66-67 — `"business": {..., "hideable": False}`, `"user": {..., "hideable": False}`; line 117-118 `if not spec["hideable"]: return False` (no DB write). `api/moderation.py:299-305` — `if action == "content_hidden": try: moderation_service.hide_content(...) except ...` — the bool return is discarded. `ReportReviewScreen.js:194-201` — Hide `<Button>` has `disabled={!!busy}` only, no `target_type`/`hideable` gate.

--- PROVENANCE (added by triage 2026-08-20) ---
Found by the sentinel cron, which audits the WORKING TREE of ~/brain/10-swingby/swingby while computing its diff range from origin/main, and never syncs the two. From 2026-08-19 21:13 until 2026-08-20 21:58 that tree sat on fix/fabricated-social-proof @ a1e9446 — FIVE commits behind main, missing #159 and #163 ('74 of 86 findings fixed with tests'). Two findings sampled from this batch (HEIC upload sniff, expiry-sweep statuses) were already fixed on main and their proofs were false there. UNVERIFIED AGAINST main — re-check before fixing.
STALE-RISK: lower — no cited file changed between a1e9446 and main.

- **fix** have `resolve_report` reject (400) `content_hidden` when `TARGETS[target_type]["hideable"]` is `False`, and disable the Hide button client-side for `business`/`user` targets.

- **status** `open` · **area** `mobile` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `mobile/src/screens/business/JobManagementScreen.js:499-501,918-922,954-957,1018-1021`
- **evidence** [WRONG NUMBERS] This diff's F090/F104 fix correctly swapped `booking.total_amount` (client's gross) for `paidToBusiness(booking)` at all three render sites, killing the old "gross charge shown as payout" bug — but `paidToBusiness()` falls back to `payment_state.amount_held` when nothing has been released yet, and all three sites still render that fallback in the same `colors.success` green as a real payout, with no distinction from the `released` case. `amount_held` is the raw pre-split escrow balance (not yet reduced by the platform's 10% cut, since the cut is only computed at release time in `compute_completion_release_cents`), so a business mid-job sees a confident green dollar figure that is both larger than what they'll actually receive and not yet theirs at all — money still sitting in escrow, indistinguishable in the UI from money already paid out.

Proof: `paidToBusiness()` (lines 918-922) — `return released > 0 ? released : held;`, and its own header comment (line 913-916) claims the render is "the business's actual take... total_amount minus SwingBy's 10% cut" — true only for the `released` branch. `backend/app/api/bookings.py::_payment_state`'s `held` branch sets `held_c = escrow.money_cents(payment, "escrow_held")` with no cut subtracted (the cut is computed later, in `release_escrow_on_complete` → `compute_completion_release_cents`). All three call sites (lines 499-501, 954-957, 1018-1021) render `${paidToBusiness(booking)}` in `{ color: colors.success, ... }` unconditionally, with no `payment_state.state` check — unlike `BusinessInvoicesScreen.js`'s `InvoiceRow`, which sources its badge/text color from `STATE_BADGES[ps.state]` specifically so a `held` (in-escrow) row reads `colors.accentText`, not green, and is never confused with a `released`/`paid_off_platform` row.

### SB-0236 — Admin table "sort by Owner/Name" scrambles rows instead of sorting, surviving today's own field-name fixes

- **status** `open` · **area** `web` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `web/admin/src/pages/BusinessesPage.jsx:288,291,`, `web/admin/src/pages/UsersPage.jsx:60,63-65`
- **evidence** [WRONG ALGORITHM] Today's diff fixed the *rendered* Owner/Name text by reading the correct nested/split fields (`biz.users.first_name/last_name`, `user.first_name/last_name`) through `ownerName()`/`displayName()`, but left each column's `key` as the old, nonexistent flat field (`owner_name`, `name`). `DataTable.jsx` sorts by `row[col.key]` directly, not through the column's `render`, so clicking either header still doesn't sort by name — worse, it corrupts row order.

Proof: `DataTable.jsx:16-21` sorts `[...data].sort((a,b) => { const av=a[sortKey], bv=b[sortKey]; if (av==null) return 1; if (bv==null) return -1; ... })`. Since no row has `row.owner_name` (`BusinessesPage.jsx:288`) or `row.name` (`UsersPage.jsx:60`) — the real data lives in `row.users.first_name/last_name` and `row.first_name/last_name` respectively — `av` is `null` for every row, so the comparator returns `1` for every pair regardless of `bv`, which is not a valid sort comparator (not antisymmetric) and reorders the table unpredictably rather than leaving it alone or sorting it.

STALE-RISK: lower — no cited file changed between a1e9446 and main.

Since no row has `row.owner_name` (`BusinessesPage.jsx:288`) or `row.name` (`UsersPage.jsx:60`) — the real data lives in `row.users.first_name/last_name` and `row.first_name/last_name` respectively — `av` is `null` for every row, so the comparator returns `1` for every pair regardless of `bv`, which is not a valid sort comparator (not antisymmetric) and reorders the table unpredictably rather than leaving it alone or sorting it.`

### SB-0240 — D-W2's "record cash/e-transfer" recovery offer never fires on the path that triggers it most, because the wrapped error it inspects loses `.response`

- **status** `open` · **area** `mobile` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `mobile/src/screens/business/JobManagementScreen.js:362-377,`, `mobile/src/services/finishJob.js:53-63`
- **evidence** [WRONG ALGORITHM] `handleAdvance`'s new catch computes `unpaid = err?.response?.status === 409 && /has not been paid.../.test(detail)` to decide whether to offer recording an off-platform payment. But when the underlying 409 happens on the *same* `finishJob()` call that just auto-submitted proof (the common one-tap "Mark complete" case, not the rarer case where proof was already sent earlier), `finishJob` catches the raw axios error and re-throws it wrapped as `new ProofSentButNotCompleteError(err?.response?.data?.detail || err?.message)` — a plain `Error` subclass that never copies `.response` onto itself. Back in the screen's catch, `err.response` is therefore `undefined`, so `status` is `undefined`, so `unpaid` is always `false` for this path — the exact "photos sent, job unpaid" dead end this diff was written to fix falls straight through to the old bare "OK" alert instead of the new cash/e-transfer offer.

Proof: `finishJob.js:106-113` — `catch (err) { if (proofSubmitted) throw new ProofSentButNotCompleteError(err?.response?.data?.detail || err?.message); throw err; }` — the new Error carries only `message`/`name`/`proofSentButNotComplete`, no `response`. `JobManagementScreen.js:362-364` — `const status = err?.response?.status; ... const unpaid = status === 409 && /has not been paid|no money to release/i.test(detail);` reads `.response` off whatever `err` it received. `:366` even has a dedicated `&& !err?.proofSentButNotComplete` clause implying the two cases were meant to be told apart, but `unpaid` is already unconditionally `false` whenever `proofSentButNotComplete` is true, so that clause is dead and the halfDone branch (`:381-386`) is the only one ever reached for this combination — with a "try again" message that will 409 the same way forever, since retrying re-enters the identical unpaid/proof-already-submitted state.

Two findings sampled from this batch (HEIC upload sniff, expiry-sweep statuses) were already fixed on main and their proofs were false there. UNVERIFIED AGAINST main — re-check before fixing.
STALE-RISK: HIGH — a cited file changed between a1e9446 and main.

### SB-0241 — "Jobs done" and the Job Management "Past" tab count different sets of bookings for the same business

- **status** `open` · **area** `backend` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `backend/app/api/businesses.py:757-767,`, `mobile/src/utils/bookingBuckets.js:44-46`
- **evidence** [WRONG NUMBERS] The public profile's `completed_bookings` stat counts only `status == 'completed'` bookings, while `JobManagementScreen`'s "Past" tab (`bucketBooking`) buckets both `completed` AND `cancelled` bookings into `'past'`. A business whose only history is cancelled jobs shows nothing on its "Jobs done" stat but "Past (N)" on its own Job Management tab — the exact "0 JOBS vs Past (4)" contradiction the 2026-08-13 walkthrough (D-W8) flagged and explicitly left unfixed as "an aggregation question in `_attach_assignee` / completed_bookings", still open in `main` today.

### SB-0242 — Employee profile's "Since" tenure for a business owner is the date they were first assigned a job, not when they joined

For a solo owner this row is materialized lazily by `_ensure_owner_employee` (referenced in commit 911fd92's D-W5 fix) "on first use", i.e. the first time the owner is assigned to a job, not when the business signed up. `EmployeeProfileScreen` renders that value unconditionally as the "Since" stat with no owner special-case, so an owner who has run their business for months but was only just auto-assigned their first job shows a brand-new "Since" date on their own profile — the same root cause 911fd92 fixed for the Job Management trust card's "New to the team" copy, but that fix never touched this endpoint or screen, and any team member tapping the owner's card in `BusinessProfileScreen`'s team grid (`navigation.navigate('EmployeeProfile', ...)`, line 1226) still reaches it.

Proof: `employees.py:469` `"joined_at": employee.get("created_at")` — no owner branch, no fallback to `businesses.created_at` or the user's signup date. `EmployeeProfileScreen.js:215-216` `jobsCompleted`/`sinceLabel` are read straight off the response; lines 301-304 render `sinceLabel` as the "Since" stat with no `is_owner` guard, unlike the fix 911fd92 applied to `JobManagementScreen.js:145` for the same underlying fact.

### SB-0243 — "Needs Action" reason and bucket never learned about the auto-confirmed preferred-date booking this same diff documented

- **status** `open` · **area** `mobile` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `mobile/src/utils/bookingBuckets.js:42-46,56`
- **evidence** [WRONG ALGORITHM] This diff rewrote bookingBuckets.js's header comment to admit that a 'confirmed' booking can already carry a real `confirmed_date` (interests.py:385 copies the client's preferred_date at accept and skips the propose/confirm handshake entirely) — and added a passing test proving `jobDate`/`bucketBooking` now recognize that. But `needsActionReason`, the sibling function that drives the actual on-screen label, was never touched: for such a booking, once the owner assigns staff via `PATCH /assign-employee` (which JobManagementScreen.js:427-429 calls with only `employee_id`, never `proposed_date_1`), `!booking.employee_id` is false and `!booking.proposed_date_1` is true, so it returns `'proposeDate'` → the "Propose a time" badge — even though the date is already locked in. `bucketBooking` also keeps such a booking in `'needsAction'` forever (status never leaves 'confirmed' on this path, since nothing calls `PATCH /confirm-date` for it), so it can never surface in Dashboard's Today/Upcoming widgets or JobManagementScreen's Today/Upcoming tabs no matter how soon the job is.

Proof: `bookingBuckets.js:42-46` `needsActionReason` — `if (!booking.employee_id) return 'unassigned'; if (!booking.proposed_date_1) return 'proposeDate'; return 'awaitingDate';` has no check for `booking.confirmed_date`. `JobManagementScreen.js:1090` renders `NEEDS_ACTION_REASON_KEY[needsActionReason(booking)]` verbatim as the card's warning badge, and `i18n.js:226` maps `proposeDate` to "Propose a time". The new test in `mobile/src/__tests__/booking-past-due.test.js` ("a confirmed booking may already carry a date") only asserts `bucketBooking(b, NOW) === 'needsAction'` and `jobDate(b)` is non-null — it never calls `needsActionReason`, so the wrong label shipped untested in the same commit that added the test file.

Two findings sampled from this batch (HEIC upload sniff, expiry-sweep statuses) were already fixed on main and their proofs were false there. UNVERIFIED AGAINST main — re-check before fixing.
STALE-RISK: HIGH — a cited file changed between a1e9446 and main.
- **verify** ``bookingBuckets.js:42-46` `needsActionReason` — `if (!booking.employee_id) return 'unassigned'; if (!booking.proposed_date_1) return 'proposeDate'; return 'awaitingDate';` has no check for `booking.confirmed_date`. `JobManagementScreen.js:1090` renders `NEEDS_ACTION_REASON_KEY[needsActionReason(booking)]` verbatim as the card's warning badge, and `i18n.js:226` maps `proposeDate` to "Propose a time". The new test in `mobile/src/__tests__/booking-past-due.test.js` ("a confirmed booking may already carry a date") only asserts `bucketBooking(b, NOW) === 'needsAction'` and `jobDate(b)` is non-null — it never calls `needsActionReason`, so the wrong label shipped untested in the same commit that added the test file.`
- **fix** give `needsActionReason` a `booking.confirmed_date` check ahead of the `proposed_date_1` one (return something like `'unassigned'` only, with no propose-a-time state, once a date already exists), and let `bucketBooking` route a 'confirmed' booking with a real `confirmed_date` into 'today'/'upcoming' the same way an 'in_progress' one is.

### SB-0244 — PostHog pageview tracking only ever fires once per session; the SPA route-change hook it was built for is never called

- **status** `open` · **area** `web` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `web/launch/src/lib/analytics.js:88,`, `web/pre-launch/src/lib/analytics.js:88`
- **evidence** [WRONG ALGORITHM] `initAnalytics()` sets `capture_pageview: false` specifically because "this is a SPA and the default listener only ever sees the first URL," and exports `trackPageview()` with the comment "Call on every SPA route change; the automatic listener cannot see them." Both `web/launch` and `web/pre-launch` are client-side-routed SPAs (`react-router-dom`, 40+ routes in `web/launch/src/App.jsx`), but `trackPageview` is never imported or called anywhere outside its own definition — so after the single manual `posthog.capture('$pageview')` in `initAnalytics()` fires on load, no subsequent in-app navigation is ever recorded as a pageview.

Proof: `grep -rn "trackPageview" web/launch/src web/pre-launch/src` matches only the two `export function trackPageview` definitions — zero call sites. `main.jsx` in both apps calls only `initAnalytics()`, with no `useLocation`-driven effect wired to `trackPageview`.

- **evidence** [COPYWRITING] `aae7765` (SB-0007) audited this exact file for English literals hard-baked into JSX/text props and added a regression test — but its guard regex only checks props named `sub|label|title|placeholder`; `actionLabel` was never in the list, so the "New opportunities" section header's "See all" link stayed a bare English string. A French/Arabic/Ukrainian business owner sees a translated screen with one English link sitting in the middle of it — the identical defect class SB-0007 was filed and fixed for, missed by one prop name.

Proof: `DashboardScreen.js:551` — `actionLabel={visiblePosts.length > 0 ? 'See all' : null}`, never `i18n.t(...)`. `SectionHeader.js:62` renders `{actionLabel}` as visible text. The new guard test (`mobile/src/__tests__/dashboard-speaks-the-users-language.test.js:77`) matches `/(?:sub|label|title|placeholder)\s*=\s*\{?\s*'.../` — `actionLabel` does not match `label` as a whole prop name (the regex requires the prop name to BE one of those words, and `actionLabel` isn't), so this line passes the new test while still being a bare literal.

### SB-0247 — D7.8's URL-safety fix only guards a user's own avatar — two other writers of the identical field/class are still wide open

- **status** `open` · **area** `backend` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `backend/app/api/employees.py:28,185,`, `backend/app/api/businesses.py:35-54,124-127,142-145`
- **evidence** [WRONG ALGORITHM] D7.8 (`693e661`) built `url_safety.validate_public_https_url` specifically because `PATCH /auth/me` accepted and stored `http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/`, `http://127.0.0.1:22`, `gopher://127.0.0.1:6379/_INFO`, etc. into `users.avatar_url` — a stored-URL vector the module's own docstring says is worth closing "at the write, where there is one place to put it, not at each future read." It was wired into exactly one writer of that column: `auth.py`'s self-service `PATCH /me` and social sign-in. `employees.py`'s `EmployeeCreate.avatar_url` writes into the SAME `users.avatar_url` column (line 185) with zero validation of any kind — not even a scheme check — so a business owner creating a teammate can set that employee's avatar to `javascript:...`, `data:...`, or any internal address. `businesses.py`'s `_validate_logo_url` is the pre-D7.8 sibling check for `businesses.logo_url` (its own docstring: "Same contract as `users.avatar_url`") — it only requires `http://` or `https://`, with no private-IP/internal-hostname/loopback rejection, and was never upgraded to call `url_safety.validate_public_https_url` when that module was built. `logo_url` is rendered on every business search result to every signed-in user, a wider blast radius than the avatar_url case the fix targeted.

Proof: `employees.py:28` `avatar_url: Optional[str] = Field(None, max_length=2048)` — no `field_validator`. `employees.py:185` `"avatar_url": data.avatar_url` written straight into the new user row. `businesses.py:52-53` `if not v.lower().startswith(("http://", "https://")): raise ValueError(...)` — accepts plain `http://` and any public or private host, unlike `url_safety.py`'s `_BLOCKED_HOSTNAMES`/`_is_private_address` checks. `grep -rn "url_safety" backend/app/api/*.py` (excluding `url_safety.py` itself) shows only `auth.py` importing it.

STALE-RISK: HIGH — a cited file changed between a1e9446 and main.

(` gates the entire block containing the new `{(post.address || post.area) ? ... : null}` at lines 168-170. ### SB-0253 — A top-up's second Stripe capture is unrefundable — payments records one intent id, not per-capture amounts

- **status** `open` · **area** `backend` · found 2026-08-21 by triage-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `backend/app/api/payments_stripe.py:853`, `backend/app/api/bookings.py:1586`, `supabase/migrations`
- **repro** Post a job with a $150 budget under Flow A (charged at post). Accept a quote for $200 — trigger_on_accept captures the $50 delta as a second PaymentIntent. Then cancel with a full-refund entitlement. Stripe is asked to refund $200 against the $150 intent, refuses, and the client is not made whole.
- **evidence** Residual of SB-0094, split out rather than half-fixed. SB-0094 stopped the DESTRUCTION — the first capture's intent id now survives a top-up instead of being overwritten by the delta capture's. But the payments table has exactly one Stripe reference column (stripe_payment_intent_id) and records no per-capture amounts, so a booking accepted above budget still has a second real Stripe capture that the database cannot address. bookings.py:1586-1593 refunds the FULL client_refund against the single stored intent; where the refund exceeds what that one capture took, Stripe refuses and the except at :1596 logs 'LEDGER SAYS REFUNDED, STRIPE DID NOT' while the client is short-changed. Today the second capture's id is preserved only in an application log line (payments_stripe.py, SECOND CAPTURE ...), which is recoverable by a human but not by code. Needs a schema change: a stripe_captures jsonb (or a payment_captures table) holding {intent_id, amount_cents} per capture, plus a refund path that walks them in order until the refund amount is satisfied. NOT done tonight because DATABASE_URL is empty on this box and the Supabase MCP was disconnected, so no migration could be APPLIED — and a filed-but-unapplied migration is a known trap here.
- **verify** `Schema: the payments table (or a payment_captures table) records an amount per capture — confirm against live Supabase, not the schema docs. Behaviour: a pytest that seeds a row with two captures (15000 + 5000) and asserts a 20000-cent refund issues TWO Stripe refund calls totalling 20000, not one call for 20000 against the first intent. Regression: the SECOND CAPTURE reconciliation log line in payments_stripe.py is no longer reachable, because both captures are now addressable.`
- **fix** Add a stripe_captures jsonb column (migration, applied before merge) appending {intent_id, amount_cents} on every capture; refund across them in order.

### SB-0028 — Cloudflare account ID and the operator's account-name email remain in published git history — the .gitignore comment claims this was closed, but untracking does not purge

- **status** `open` · **area** `infra` · found 2026-08-19 by audit-wf-72374f5f · checked never re-verified
- **files** `.gitignore ("Cloudflare Wrangler local state" block)`, `backend/.wrangler/cache/wrangler-account.json @4820da5 (blob 1489ba283a22f1862876d537ca4e0198764521e0)`, `workers/waitlist/.wrangler/cache/wrangler-account.json @4820da5 (blob 819ae4d316bdd8502d3ea661fdccb2816639fb8b)`
- **evidence** [overnight audit resume wf_72374f5f — dimension: secrets and credential hygiene] code_state=committed confidence=confirmed NEEDS-HUMAN

Four wrangler cache files were committed in 4820da5 (2026-05-22) and only untracked in e7a5ee2:
  A  backend/.wrangler/cache/pages.json
  A  backend/.wrangler/cache/wrangler-account.json
  A  workers/waitlist/.wrangler/cache/pages.json
  A  workers/waitlist/.wrangler/cache/wrangler-account.json

Structural inspection of the committed blobs (fields only, no values printed):
  backend/.wrangler/cache/wrangler-account.json
    /account/id    type=str len=32 kind=HEX32
    /account/name  type=str len=30 kind=EMAIL
  workers/waitlist/.wrangler/cache/wrangler-account.json
    /account/id    type=str len=32 kind=HEX32
    /account/name  type=str len=29 kind=EMAIL
  both pages.json: /account_id  len=32 kind=HEX32
No API token is present in any of the four — that is the good news.

commit 4820da5 is an ancestor of origin/main (verified), and the repo is PUBLIC.

The .gitignore text asserts the problem is handled:
  # Cache files carrying the Cloudflare account ID and the account-name email.
  # Four of these were tracked before this rule existed; they are untracked in the
  # same commit that adds it. This repo is PUBLIC — none of it should be readable.
That is true of HEAD and false of history.

CONSEQUENCE: Low direct impact — no token leaked, and the same Cloudflare Account ID (4877404e65143359d52e1056bfd8099c) and Zone ID are already published in the committed CLAUDE.md, so this adds only the operator's email address as a fresh disclosure (useful for targeted phishing against the Cloudflare account). The real cost is the misleading comment: a future reader takes "untracked in the same commit" as remediation and does not check history, which is exactly the reasoning error that left the Maps key in finding #1 unaddressed.
- **verify** `cd /home/l3thal/brain/10-swingby/swingby && git log --all --format=%H --find-object=1489ba283a22f1862876d537ca4e0198764521e0 | head -1
→ must output nothing after a history purge. If the decision is "accept, do not rewrite", then instead: grep -q 'remain in published history' .gitignore  → must succeed.`
- **fix** Either purge in the same git-filter-repo pass as the Maps key (finding #1) — one rewrite, one force-push, both problems — or, if history is being left alone, amend the .gitignore comment to say plainly "these four blobs remain in published history; the ignore rule only prevents new ones" so nobody re-reads it as closed. Enable 2FA + a hardware key on the Cloudflare account given the email is public.

### SB-0040 — Analytics collects nothing in production — VITE_POSTHOG_KEY is not set in the build

- **status** `confirmed` · **area** `infra` · found 2026-08-19 by audit-wf-72374f5f · checked 2026-08-19
- **files** `docs/DEPLOY.md:218`, `docs/DEPLOY.md:234`, `docs/RUNNING_LOCALLY.md:68`, `.github/workflows/web-launch-ci.yml:46`, `web/launch/.env.example:11`, `web/pre-launch/src/lib/analytics.js:24`
- **evidence** [overnight audit resume wf_72374f5f — dimension: external integrations — Cloudflare, Supabase, Render, Stripe] code_state=committed confidence=confirmed NEEDS-HUMAN

Deployed bundle: `grep -c -i posthog main.js` -> 0, and `grep -oE "phc_[A-Za-z0-9]{10,}" main.js` -> nothing. The snippet is not merely inert, it was tree-shaken out, which only happens when Vite statically substitutes an empty `import.meta.env.VITE_POSTHOG_KEY` at build time.

The call site is wired: web/pre-launch/src/main.jsx:10 `import { initAnalytics } from './lib/analytics'` and main.jsx:14 `initAnalytics()`. The gate is analytics.js:22-25:
  const KEY = import.meta.env.VITE_POSTHOG_KEY
  export function initAnalytics() { if (!KEY) return

The deploy runbook never mentions the variable. docs/DEPLOY.md:218 (frontend env-var table for web/launch):
  | `VITE_PLAUSIBLE_DOMAIN` | `swingbyy.com` |
and DEPLOY.md:234 in the go-live checklist:
  - [ ] Plausible domain configured
No VITE_POSTHOG_KEY row exists in either. Same in docs/RUNNING_LOCALLY.md:68 (`VITE_PLAUSIBLE_DOMAIN=    # leave empty locally`), web/launch/.env.example:11 (`# Analytics — Plausible (optional)`), and .github/workflows/web-launch-ci.yml:46 which sets `VITE_PLAUSIBLE_DOMAIN: swingbyy.com` and no PostHog var, so CI has never built the analytics path even once.

CONSEQUENCE: Commits 6cd2c5a/b5ff619 migrated code, CSP and subprocessor docs to PostHog on 2026-08-15, but the operator-facing half never moved. Anyone configuring a deploy from DEPLOY.md sets a variable nothing reads and omits the one thing that turns analytics on — and the failure is silent by design (`if (!KEY) return`). Production has been collecting zero funnel data since the migration, which is the data the October launch decisions are supposed to rest on. Note this is separate from the CSP finding: the CSP already allows the PostHog hosts, so fixing the key alone will work.
- **verify** `curl -s https://swingbyy.com/$(curl -s https://swingbyy.com/ | grep -oE 'assets/index-[A-Za-z0-9_-]+\.js' | head -1) | grep -c posthog   — must be > 0. Then confirm an event actually lands in the PostHog project; a key in the bundle only proves the snippet loaded.`
- **fix** Add VITE_POSTHOG_KEY (and optionally VITE_POSTHOG_HOST) to web/pre-launch/.env.production, which is TRACKED and is what the deploy builds from. Then redeploy and confirm events arrive in PostHog.
- _note_ 2026-08-19: ADVERSARIAL VERIFY 2026-08-19 (wf_40a8b5fc): SURVIVED refutation. corrected_severity=low. Stored verify step judged inadequate and replaced. I tried hard to break this one and the CORE FACT survived — but almost every supporting claim around it is wrong, and "blocker" is inflated by three levels.

WHAT I CONFIRMED (independently, not by trusting the auditor):
1. The live bundle really has no PostHog. `curl -s https://swingbyy.com/` names five app chunks; I fetched the entry chunk `https://swingbyy.com/assets/index-CsH_uuQv.js` (HTTP 200, 483877 bytes) and `grep -c -i posthog` -> **0**, `grep -oE 'phc_[A-Za-z0-9]{10,}'` -> nothing, `grep -c -i plausible` -> 0. I also fetched all four vendor chunks (vendor-i18n / vendor-motion / vendor-react / vendor-supabase): posthog=0 in every one. So it is not hiding in a split chunk.
2. The deployed build IS post-migration, which I had to prove because "the build is just old" was my best refutation. `curl -sI https://swingbyy.com/` returns `content-security-policy: ... script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline' https://us-assets.i.posthog.com https://eu-assets.i.posthog.com; ... connect-src ... https://us.i.posthog.com ...`. That header comes from web/pre-launch/public/_headers, and `git show b5ff619~1:web/pre-launch/public/_headers | grep -c posthog` -> 0, i.e. the PostHog hosts entered that file only in b5ff619 (2026-08-15). Live edge is serving the post-migration _headers with a bundle that has no PostHog in it. Confirmed.
3. Docs really are stale: docs/DEPLOY.md:218 `| \`VITE_PLAUSIBLE_DOMAIN\` | \`swingbyy.com\` |`, DEPLOY.md:234 `- [ ] Plausible domain configured`, docs/RUNNING_LOCALLY.md:68 `VITE_PLAUSIBLE_DOMAIN=    # leave empty locally`, web/launch/.env.example:11-12, .github/workflows/web-launch-ci.yml:46 `VITE_PLAUSIBLE_DOMAIN: swingbyy.com`. All present at HEAD 5c1b029, all COMMITTED (not WIP — `git status` shows none of these files modified).

WHAT IS WRONG WITH THE FINDING AS WRITTEN — four errors:

(a) THE HEADLINE PROOF GREPPED THE WRONG FILE. Evidence says "Deployed bundle: `grep -c -i posthog main.js` -> 0". There is no `main.js` in this app — its chunks are `assets/index-<hash>.js`. The only `main.js` referenced by swingbyy.com's HTML is `/cdn-cgi/challenge-platform/scripts/jsd/main.js`, Cloudflare's bot-challenge script. The auditor almost certainly grepped Cloudflare's script and read it as the app bundle. The conclusion happens to be right — I re-derived it against the real entry chunk — but the stated proof does not support it, and the "it was t
- _note_ 2026-08-19: Adversarially verified 2026-08-19 (wf_40a8b5fc): core mechanism reproduces, but severity/consequence were overstated — downgraded. Re-read the title against the verdict note before fixing.
- _note_ 2026-08-19: DOC HALF FIXED in 516d1dc: DEPLOY.md and RUNNING_LOCALLY.md no longer tell operators to set VITE_PLAUSIBLE_DOMAIN. Stays OPEN for the real finding — the deployed bundle contains no PostHog code, which needs a rebuild/redeploy and a human to confirm events arrive.
- _note_ 2026-08-20: DIAGNOSIS CORRECTED 2026-08-20. The original wording ('deployed bundle contains no PostHog code') reads as missing code. The code is present and correct: src/lib/analytics.js loads PostHog from the CDN via the official snippet and starts with , so a build with no key deliberately ships nothing. posthog-js is intentionally NOT an npm dependency — that is documented in the file. The single cause is that VITE_POSTHOG_KEY is absent from web/pre-launch/.env.production, and the deploy workflow deliberately injects no env (an unset GitHub secret expands to an empty string and would OVERRIDE .env.production). Re-verified after tonight's deploy: 0 posthog references in the live bundle. Needs the real project key, which only a human has. The docs half — DEPLOY.md and RUNNING_LOCALLY.md telling operators to set the dead VITE_PLAUSIBLE_DOMAIN — is already fixed.

### SB-0120 — Blocked-thread composer stays live and its purpose-built refusal copy is never shown

When the inevitable send does fail (blocked-pair 403, or a content-moderation BLOCK from `content_moderation.screen_text`), `handleSend`'s `catch` block is a bare `catch { … }` that only drops the optimistic bubble and restores the draft — it never reads the error at all, so the specific, three-locale-translated `moderation.messageRefused` / `moderation.threadBlockedBody` copy is dead code; whatever the user actually sees comes from the generic global axios interceptor's "Request failed" toast (`services/api.js` `extractMessage`), which surfaces the backend's hardcoded-English `detail` string regardless of the app's locale.

Proof: `ChatScreen.js:1363` `disabled={!text.trim() || sending}` (no `threadBlocked`). `ChatScreen.js:574-578` `catch { setMessages(...); setText(trimmed); }` — no branch on `err`. `git grep -n "moderation.messageRefused" mobile/src` returns only its three i18n.js definitions (1265, 1339, 1408) — zero render call sites.

### SB-0150 — GET /reviews/client/{id} embeds a relationship the schema doesn't have, so it errors on every call

- **status** `open` · **area** `backend` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified

- **evidence** [WRONG ALGORITHM] `get_client_reviews` asks PostgREST to embed `businesses(business_name, logo_url)` off `reviews.reviewee_id`, but `reviewee_id` is a `users.id` when `reviewee_type='client'` (per `docs/swingby_database_schema.md`'s own note that the column is "polymorphic... Not a clean FK to one table"). There is no FK path from `reviews` to `businesses` via `reviewee_id` for a client-type row, so the embed fails and the except-block turns it into a generic 400 on every call. The two sibling functions in the same file get this right, embedding `users(first_name, last_name)` via the real `reviewer_id` FK. No caller currently exists anywhere in `mobile/src` or `web/*/src`, so this has no live user-facing impact today, but it will break the instant a screen wires up to it.

Proof: `reviews.py:196` `.select("*, businesses(business_name, logo_url)")` with `.eq("reviewee_id", client_id).eq("reviewee_type", "client")`; contrast `reviews.py:178` and `:224`, both `.select("*, users(first_name, last_name)")`. `docs/swingby_database_schema.md:251` — "Not a clean FK to one table."

Two findings sampled from this batch (HEIC upload sniff, expiry-sweep statuses) were already fixed on main and their proofs were false there. UNVERIFIED AGAINST main — re-check before fixing.

### SB-0192 — payouts.summarize() duplicates available_cents() logic and drops its safety log

- **status** `open` · **area** `backend` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `backend/app/services/payouts.py:108-129,132-149`
- **evidence** [WRONG ALGORITHM] `available_cents()` clamps a negative balance to 0 and logs a WARNING so a "more paid out than earned" reconciliation problem "surfaces somewhere it can be investigated rather than being silently clamped" (its own docstring, line 114). `summarize()` — the function that actually powers the wallet screen a business owner checks routinely (`GET /me/payouts`) — recomputes the same `earned - sent` clamp inline instead of calling `available_cents()`, so the exact failure mode the docstring warns about is silently swallowed on every wallet read; the WARNING only fires when a business happens to hit "cash out" (the other caller of `available_cents()`).

Proof: `available_cents()` (payouts.py:117-129) does `net = earned - sent; if net < 0: logger.warning(...); return 0`. `summarize()` (payouts.py:142-145) instead does `"available_cents": max(earned - sent, 0)` — same arithmetic, no warning, and not even a call to `available_cents()`.

### SB-0217 — Booking Details price pill's off-platform case is dead code — it reads a field the backend never sets to that value

- **status** `open` · **area** `mobile` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified

The case can never fire; an off-platform-paid booking's pill shows the generic "PAID" label instead.

Proof: `payments_offplatform.py:132` — `supabase.table("bookings").update({"payment_status": "fully_released"}).eq("id", booking_id)`. Repo-wide grep for `payment_status` assignments to `"paid_off_platform"` across `backend/app` returns nothing. `BookingDetailsScreen.js:1073` calls `paymentPillLabel(booking?.payment_status)`, never `payment?.status` (the field that actually does hold `"paid_off_platform"`).

### SB-0232 — A foreground push that's later tapped creates two Notifications-Center entries, one permanently stuck unread

- **status** `open` · **area** `mobile` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `mobile/src/services/notifications.js:97-145`
- **evidence** [WRONG NUMBERS] `persistNotification` is called from both `handleNotificationReceived` (fires when a push arrives while the app is running) and `handleNotificationResponse` (fires on tap), with no de-duplication against the underlying notification's own identifier — each call mints a fresh `id` via `Date.now()-Math.random()`. A push that arrives while the app is foregrounded/backgrounded-but-alive, then gets tapped later from the OS tray, is persisted twice: once by the received listener (`read: false`) and again by the response listener (`read: true`), leaving two rows in NotificationsCenterScreen for one real notification — the `read: false` copy has no tap target left to mark it read (its own copy already got a new id) and sits in the list forever.

Proof: `notifications.js:122-124` `function handleNotificationReceived(notification) { persistNotification(notification?.request?.content, { read: false }); }` and `:136-145`'s `handleNotificationResponse` also calls `persistNotification(content, { read: true })` for the same tap — `persistNotification` (lines 97-120) always pushes a new object with a freshly generated `id` (line 101), never checking `list` for an existing entry with the same `data.booking_id`/`data.event_type` before appending. `notifications.test.js` only exercises each listener in isolation (never both for the same notification), so this path is untested.

--- PROVENANCE (added by triage 2026-08-20) ---
Found by the sentinel cron, which audits the WORKING TREE of ~/brain/10-swingby/swingby while computing its diff range from origin/main, and never syncs the two. From 2026-08-19 21:13 until 2026-08-20 21:58 that tree sat on fix/fabricated-social-proof @ a1e9446 — FIVE commits behind main, missing #159 and #163 ('74 of 86 findings fixed with tests'). Two findings sampled from this batch (HEIC upload sniff, expiry-sweep statuses) were already fixed on main and their proofs were false there. UNVERIFIED AGAINST main — re-check before fixing.
STALE-RISK: lower — no cited file changed between a1e9446 and main.
- **verify** ``notifications.js:122-124` `function handleNotificationReceived(notification) { persistNotification(notification?.request?.content, { read: false }); }` and `:136-145`'s `handleNotificationResponse` also calls `persistNotification(content, { read: true })` for the same tap — `persistNotification` (lines 97-120) always pushes a new object with a freshly generated `id` (line 101), never checking `list` for an existing entry with the same `data.booking_id`/`data.event_type` before appending. `notifications.test.js` only exercises each listener in isolation (never both for the same notification), so this path is untested.`
- **fix** before appending in `persistNotification`, check whether an entry with the same `meta.bookingId`/`type` already exists in `list` (from the received copy) and update it in place (mark read, don't duplicate) instead of always pushing a new row.

### SB-0245 — Quote comparison hint states the wrong sort operator — code divides, copy says multiply

- **status** `open` · **area** `mobile` · found 2026-08-21 by sentinel-2026-08-20 · checked never re-verified
- **files** `mobile/src/screens/client/QuoteComparisonScreen.js:238-242,409`
- **evidence** [COPYWRITING] The on-screen hint above the quote list tells the client quotes are "Sorted by best rating × price," but the code computes each quote's score as `rating / price` (a rating-per-dollar ratio, where a lower price raises the score), not a product. A client reading the hint would conclude a higher price pushes a quote up the list — the opposite of what actually happens.

Proof: `QuoteComparisonScreen.js:238` `// sort: score = rating / price (higher is better); rating nests under businesses`, with `:240-241` `const scoreA = (a.businesses?.avg_rating || 0) / (a.quoted_price || 1); const scoreB = (b.businesses?.avg_rating || 0) / (b.quoted_price || 1);`. `:409` renders `Sorted by best rating × price. Tap a name to view profile.` on the same screen.

Tap a name to view profile.` on the same screen.`
